ParlaMint-AT_1996-05-07-020-XX-NRSITZ-00020_d7e3235	We're now on the 1st. ParlaMint-AT_1996-05-07-020-XX-NRSITZ-00020_d7e3262	Mr President. Ladies and gentlemen! High house! In the 1990s, the global economic environment changed significantly. The strong economic downturn in 1992/93, the internationally flattened growth and, above all, the emergence of new, very strong competitors on world markets have put virtually all industrialized countries in difficulty and led to a strong increase in unemployment. In Switzerland, unemployment rose from 0.5 percent to 4.7 percent between 1990 and 1995, in Germany by almost 3 percentage points from 6.9 percent to 9.6 percent, in Spain from 17.3 percent to 24.2 percent, and in Sweden from 1.4 percent to 8 percent. Ladies and gentlemen! Of course, Austria could not completely escape this international development either. Austria has, however, succeeded in keeping growth significantly higher and unemployment growth significantly lower during this period of time. [[Congratulations with ÖVP and SPÖ.]] According to OECD Statistics 1995, only Japan, Luxembourg and Austria have succeeded in keeping the unemployment rate below 4.5 percent. Ladies and gentlemen! This fact should be a reason for courage and optimism that our country will be able to avoid the technical resources that business potentials have in the future, even in the future, towards unemployment in Austria. [[Congratulations with ÖVP and SPÖ.]] In the logical consequence of this fact, the employment and economic concept of the Austrian Federal Government does not rely on a wage ban, nor on permanent debt financing, but on the national and international competitive strengths of the domestic economy. In this context, the Austrian Federal Government is focusing on export, innovation and cost reductions through debureaucratisation, flexibility and the reduction of unnecessary wage additional costs. [[Congratulations with ÖVP and SPÖ.]] [[Abg. Ing. Reichhold: Tags, nothing but keywords!]] The Austrian Federal Government does not consider linear working time reductions to be useful, but supports the social partners in their efforts for new working time models that integrate further training into our economic system, which significantly reduce seasonal unemployment and thus also initiate new employment opportunities. [[Congratulations with ÖVP and SPÖ.]] I am convinced that we will be able, with flexible conditions, to implement deregulations, to implement the increased productivity rate in jobs and to prevent defensive rationalisation concepts. Ladies and gentlemen! Austria's prospects for the future lie in a competitive productive sector that benefits from its opportunities on the world market and, in particular, on the European internal market. Austria's prospects for the future, however, are also in a highly expandable, employment-intensive sector of industrial services and in a creative leisure industry that recognises new trends and opportunities for development and transforms them into jobs. Workplaces, my honours Ladies and gentlemen, there can be no regulation, and there is no patent recipe for job creation. The objective of the Austrian Federal Government is, however, to shape the economic conditions in such a way that economic change is promoted and guided into the right track. It is the art of an economically successful and socially responsible policy that this change is accelerated, but also designed in such a way that there are no social shocks in our country and that friction losses are prevented as far as possible by strikes or radicalizations. [[Congratulations with the ÖVP and with representatives of the SPÖ.]] I think this has been better achieved in Austria up to now than in most industrialized countries in the world. Ladies and gentlemen! The Federal Government's economic and employment concept is based on clear principles and guidelines: First principle: Savings in the public sector do not endanger jobs, but rather safeguard jobs in the medium term. In this context, the successful adoption of the 100-billion schilling package is essential for the recovery of economic policy scope in order to be able to set the necessary impetus in economic and social policy as well as new initiatives in the future. I believe that the savings package provides the basis for the medium-term discharges that are necessary. [[Congratulations with the ÖVP and with representatives of the SPÖ.]] The development in other countries, especially in Sweden, has made very clear what happens if a state cannot be rescaled and restructured in time. There unemployment has increased – with the savings package of the Austrian Federal Government, this path for Austria is avoided. The recognition of this path by the International Monetary Fund has now been very clear to us that this package has brought about a gain in stability and not a loss of stability. As Minister of Economic Affairs, it is essential and important to me to note here today that the form of the implementation of tax changes does not endanger the attractiveness of Austria's economic location. On the contrary: The Austrian corporate tax system is still much more attractive than that of our neighbor Germany. [[Congratulations with ÖVP and SPÖ.]] In addition, by fixing a research billion, increasing the tax exemption, various impulses in the energy saving and construction sector, growth and employment policy initiatives were launched without endangering the savings target. This brings me to the second principle of the Austrian Federal Government: We want to provide a medium-term planning basis for those economic sectors dependent on public procurement. In the field of construction, this has been achieved by establishing a medium-term binding building and civil engineering programme. And I can say that this measure has already improved the economic and investment climate. Thanks to new financial instruments such as toll, road pricing and the dedicated sale of properties, companies can expect additional public investment not only for a year but for the next few years. The infrastructure offensive launched by the Austrian Federal Government in civil and civil engineering may not avoid any insolvency, but it will help to stabilise the construction industry as a whole as a sector and ensure that jobs are safe in the future. [[Congratulations with ÖVP and SPÖ.]] High house! Ladies and gentlemen! In this context, I call on the social partners to decide now on the largely negotiated new working time model to ensure continuous annual employment in order to ensure continuous employment and to ensure that, in the next winter, not only 40 percent but 80 to 90 percent of Austrian workers in the construction industry are employed. If we succeed, then we can save unemployment funds in billions, then we can and will set new impulses and at the same time save ourselves the annual horror reports about the high winter unemployment that the economic climate is always burdening. [[Further congratulation with ÖVP and SPÖ.]] A second essential area which currently faces structural change is tourism. It is also necessary to make discharges. But this is not about public procurement, but the most important discharge in this area is red tape. A better back cover in international competition is also important. [[Congratulations with the ÖVP and with representatives of the SPÖ.]] But I also believe that instead of permanently reiterating the crisis again today, it is urgently necessary to take a new advertising and marketing concept to Austria and thus bring new visitors to Austria. [[Congratulations with ÖVP and SPÖ.]] It is important to support companies in capital formation and to make new offers in support policy. I say it quite openly: In the current situation tax relief is not possible. But an attractive form of tax breaks in the gastronomy sector could help us to re motivate thousands of innkeepers. I therefore believe that this model should not fail in small-scale bureaucratic concerns. [[Congratulations with ÖVP and SPÖ.]] Another key point to safeguarding jobs in tourism is the facilitation of the transfer of businesses and the reduction of the conditions which then become due. In this respect too, my House will draw up an appropriate initiative before the summer. [[New applause with ÖVP and SPÖ.]] Improving income opportunities in tourism also means offering generous shop opening times in tourist areas. I am very glad that almost all the Councils of State could be won in order to establish generous opening hours for the coming summer. I hope that Vienna, too, recognises that the use of opportunities in urban tourism is only possible, that ensuring the competitive advantages to Prague is guaranteed only if we open the shops – but not if we keep them closed for too long! [[Congratulations with the ÖVP and with representatives of the SPÖ.]] High house! Ladies and gentlemen! However, the most important key to reconquering full employment is undoubtedly export and internationalization. If the Austrian economy, workers and entrepreneurs are able to secure a slightly larger market share in the internal market, on the world market, this means additional jobs in Austria. I therefore think that it is completely wrong to raise the EU to a political counterpart rather than to use the many opportunities in this European Union openly and self-confidently. [[Congratulations with ÖVP and SPÖ.]] Strengthened direct investment abroad, enhanced partnerships with foreign companies are a prerequisite for further increasing exports. In order to safeguard jobs and income, the Ministry of Economic Affairs is currently developing, together with the stakeholders, a new outline of external economic policy in order to make our export opportunities even better, even more systematic, in the coming years. This means, in concrete terms, that: We also want to allow small businesses access to the internal market through service and consultation, in particular by foreign trade agencies. According to input/output analysis by the Economic Research Institute, each additional export billion leads to 1 000 additional jobs. In 1995, Austria exported goods worth 564 billion schilling. Any further increase therefore means additional jobs. The internationalisation rate of the Austrian economy has increased as a result of the eastern opening, but it is still below the values of comparable states. We want to ensure, through better and more efficient use of European information networks, that cross-border cooperation is further promoted, supported and initiated. Internationalisation partnerships, particularly in the field of small and medium-sized enterprises, should be particularly promoted in this context. [[Congratulations with ÖVP and SPÖ.]] EU accession should be systematically used to achieve market share gains in Western and Eastern Europe. The increase in backflows from EU programmes through participation in PHARE and TACIS and consultants Trust Funds is and will be systematically increased. Through efficient coordination and a skillful lobbying, we are currently trying to benefit the European research programmes even more than ever from Austria. We also consider increasing participation in EU major projects of international development banks. This, ladies and gentlemen, requires hard work, Austrian know-how and lobbying in a positive sense. The cooperation of product producers and service providers in the export of Austrian products is currently being systematically improved and an ongoing optimization of export financing and guarantee policies is being undertaken. High house! However, the best external economic policy is undoubtedly an attractive location policy that enables local companies to compete in international competition to develop, produce and sell attractive products with a high share of value in Austria. In this context, it is also necessary to further ensure the promotion of capital formation. In Austria – and this too will be discussed again today – I believe that the proposals on injustice always focus on tax law, because in tax law, the Austrian Federal Government has already created excellent framework conditions with the abolition of property tax, the abolition of commercial income tax, the reduction of tax rates, with specific measures, such as final taxation of interest in the company, international boxing privilege. [[Congratulations with ÖVP and SPÖ.]] [[Prolonged calls to the liberals.]] As a result of these measures – and this too must be noted – the equity ratio, which in the 1970s amounted to 28 percent, has fallen to 16.5 percent in 1982, now again above the 30 percent mark. This is a good value, but we want to and we can further improve it if we increase the profits of businesses and thus allow equity to be created from within. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP.]] [[Intervocations with the liberals.]] That requires, my dear. Ladies and gentlemen, the FPÖ, the reduction of red tape costs, better machine life and a comprehensive stopover in the social security sector, to which the Austrian Federal Government acknowledges itself. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP.]] [[Further calls to the liberals.]] In addition, comprehensive external equity-building initiatives were launched by the government and the individual ministries. In my Ministry of Economic Affairs, it becomes very concrete from 1st. The capital formation in small enterprises was promoted by the BÜRGES on 1 July. In my ministry it becomes quite concrete from 1st. In July, the Company Savings Action was carried out. All of this will help us to motivate young people to become self-employed. [[Congratulations with ÖVP and SPÖ.]] For medium-sized enterprises, the capital issue shall be provided by the financing guarantee company and the SME financing companies. It is even stronger for large firms than before to make the way to the stock exchange, with the recent privatisations in the field of nationalized industry very clear that this path can be successfully pursued. High house! In my view, the fifth guideline of a forward-looking employment policy is the facilitation of business start-ups and the faster approval of plant facilities. The largest job creator was in the past and will continue to be small and medium-sized enterprises in the future. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP.]] [[Abg. Ing. Reichhold: But you have the wrong partner!]] [[President Dr. Fischer gives the bell sign.]] For these in particular, the greatest incentive lies, not in the demands of freedom, but in the creation of jobs through a radical red tape reduction programme. [[Violent calls to the liberals.]] But I say quite openly, my dear. Ladies and gentlemen: The demand for such a crash programme has been raised very quickly, but overall implementation is extremely difficult because all departments are affected and because, of course, there is a specific interest behind any complex regulation. [[Abg. Ing. Reichhold: Because you have the wrong partner!]] For me, red tape also means examining, within the framework of social, labour and environmental laws, whether the costs will also bring the appropriate benefits – or whether the costs are not worth noting. Changes are certainly only possible in the course of the constructive cooperation of the ministers of the resort. However, ladies and gentlemen, in this context, there is real expertise and a clear rejection of irrationality and populism. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP.]] [[Abg. Ing. Reichhold: You don't do it!]] In any event, the Ministry of Economic Affairs will initiate a comprehensive process acceleration in the field of plant rights. We want to achieve this by extending the simplified authorisation procedure, by enabling the application procedure and by implementing a process concentration in the normal procedure. However, the ideal objective, ladies and gentlemen, which is always called for, is to concentrate all the approval procedures on an authority that implements the overall decision, is only possible if it succeeds in ensuring the involvement of the countries, and if a constitutional provision is agreed in this context. At any rate, I will seek a concentration of federal proceedings from my department and send the relevant laws into review before the summer. In order to achieve a rapid settlement of procedures, it is also necessary to increase the use of non-official experts. The aim is to limit the procedures for the approval of installations and to make a decision within three months. In my House, too, a concept for liberalising trade regulations is being developed which reduces unnecessary tests and barriers to entry, but at the same time ensures quality. [[Congratulations with the ÖVP and with representatives of the SPÖ.]] I personally welcome the repeated demand to examine existing laws from a given date on their sense of meaning and necessity and, if necessary, to abolish them. I am deeply convinced that initiatives in this area can also be put in place in the House itself. Sixth point: The guarantee and creation of jobs also requires optimum and cost-effective supply of infrastructure to the economy in the telecommunications and energy sectors. In this context, it is necessary to increase the exposure of protected sectors to competition and to increase the efficiency of businesses. In this context, the Ministry of Economic Affairs will liberalise the Austrian electricity market, stimulate competition between the ETUs in the interests of the economy and citizens and strengthen the control of compliance with the competitive environment. [[Congratulations with the ÖVP and with representatives of the SPÖ.]] Privatisations are to be made possible, and Austrian cooperations are to be supported, with the stated objective of ensuring a co-existence and no interdependence between the Austrian electricity industry. High house! We must be careful about labelling key areas. However, I believe that telecommunications will become a central economic area of the next millennium. The applications of telecommunications services in individual enterprises – including small and medium-sized enterprises – will decide on the competitiveness of these companies and the use of telecommunications services will lead to new services and many new jobs. It is therefore very essential and important that this telecommunication development will not fall asleep. If you sleep this development, the future will fall asleep. [[Congratulations with the ÖVP and with representatives of the SPÖ.]] The Austrian Federal Government will ensure the best possible supply of telecommunications services through the constructive cooperation of finance, economics and transport ministers. In the course of the technology transfer, my department will promote the application of the technologies in particular. The Minister of Transport will liberalise the system as a whole and ensure that the rules of competition are also made and complied with through an independent regulatory authority. The Minister of Finance will do everything in the course of the disbursement, so that the Austrian Post creates the change from the state authority to the private, modern service company. This change is essential for Austria, so, ladies and gentlemen, it is a national concern in the interests of the economy, but also in the interest of the jobs of the Federal officials of the Post Office. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP.]] Last but not least, the seventh priority, as the seventh guideline, is to prevent youth unemployment by strengthening dual training. The quality of Austrian skilled workers and a well-trained youth are Austria's biggest site advantage for the future, and this competitive advantage must be further safeguarded. [[Congratulations with the ÖVP and with representatives of the SPÖ.]] [[Intervocations with the liberals.]] [[President Dr. Fischer gives the bell sign.]] Through new ways of continuing education and training, through closer cooperation between school policies, in particular specialised schools and economic practices, with enterprises, it is important to further develop this competitive advantage. High house! It must already be thought that at present more and more large firms are giving up apprenticeship training for cost reasons and, as a result, recruiting well-trained apprentices from small businesses. With immediate measures to increase the willingness to train, a general improvement in the attractiveness of dual training, a better networking of the various educational channels, the best possible integration of apprenticeships into our dual training system and the admission of new high-tech careers, we will ensure, ladies and gentlemen, that youth unemployment does not arise in Austria even in the future. [[Congratulations with ÖVP and SPÖ.]] We will ensure that, in future, qualified professionals, dynamic managers and successful entrepreneurs will be trained. In conclusion: Work for Austria, my dear friends Ladies and gentlemen of the Freedom Party, cannot be achieved by political licensing. [[Congratulations with the ÖVP and with representatives of the SPÖ.]] It can only be secured by a dynamic economy that is huge internationally. Work for Austria requires a new, more flexible working time and increased structural change in the protected areas. The seven reform lines outlined here take this development into account by the Austrian Federal Government and create a framework for a strong economy in the coming years. I am convinced that, if these lines are implemented consistently, it will be possible to exceed the economic researchers' forecasts and to create more jobs in Austria than is currently assumed. [[Congratulations with ÖVP and SPÖ.]] I am convinced that Austria will continue to succeed in the future, despite a tough competitive situation, in securing income and jobs for all Austrians. Thank you.
ParlaMint-AT_1996-05-07-020-XX-NRSITZ-00020_d7e3581	I would like to thank the Federal Minister for his statement and now give the floor to the Federal Minister for Labour and Social Affairs. - Please, Minister of State.
ParlaMint-AT_1996-05-07-020-XX-NRSITZ-00020_d7e3602	Mr President. Ladies and gentlemen! The biggest challenge for Europe, and thus for us too, is certainly employment policy in the coming years, in the pursuit of full employment; Mr Ditz has already done a lot. Clear: Laws and regulations alone did not allow employment policies to be dealt with, and it would also be bad if we had been waiting until today to take appropriate measures. We have seen in the last decades in Austria as a priority in all political fields the safeguarding of employment, full employment. Even in the difficult nineties, which are marked internationally by very dynamic structural changes in the technical field, in the field of the development of new economic areas, the opening of the eastern borders, integration in the Western European region, globalisation, Austria, unlike other countries, has managed not to lose jobs, but since 1990 has increased the number of self-employed workers in Austria: from 2.9 to over 3 million. And that in the difficult nineties! [[Congratulations with SPÖ and ÖVP.]] We therefore have a relatively favourable situation today, compared with the other states of the European Union – but I stress from the outset: we must not calm down and that cannot calm us down. According to the EU directives, the seasonally adjusted unemployment rate in Austria is currently 4 per cent, and unfortunately the European Union averages 11 per cent. And in the U.S., which have been praised again and again in recent times as a job wonderland, the unemployment rate is 5.5 percent according to these standards. Austria therefore has a good position, internationally compared. But again: This cannot calm us, we must intensify and continue the efforts of recent years to achieve full employment in the longer term. [[Congratulations with the SPÖ and representatives of the ÖVP.]] There will be no patent recipe, not a single measure with which we achieve this objective, but, as in the past, it is a sum of a whole range of measures to be pursued and implemented by the Federal Government, together with the social partners, with the federal states, with the municipalities. But first and foremost, this great success in the field of employment security is attributed to the Austrians themselves, because we have had or have such a good position in Austria in the last decades is primarily the high quality of the work of the Austrians and their dynamics to make changes. This has resulted in Austria, which in the last third of Europe in the 1970s, being one of the leaders in Europe and the world today, being economic power and prosperity. [[Congratulations with the SPÖ and representatives of the ÖVP.]] This has been achieved by the diligence, reliability and quality of the work of the Austrians – and, of course, by the good social partnership. Recently, the issue of wages and competitiveness has been discussed. One objective is to: In order to achieve good economic development, the dynamic social partnership has also contributed with a view to changes, with a view to wage closures. And these pay statements, which have always been reached together in recent years and decades, are not to be destroyed and questioned, because Austria cannot keep competitiveness with wage and social dumping in the future, but by continuing the way of quality and creating the appropriate foundations, including at law level, for the further qualifications of Austria's economic location. [[Congratulations with the SPÖ and representatives of the ÖVP.]] The social partners have also given attention to the fact that there is a social peace in Austria, as it is hardly found elsewhere. This has again proved to be necessary in the difficult measures needed to consolidate our budget, so that we can secure employment policy again in the future. In no other country has there been such a great deal of understanding of such reasonable measures, which were also agreed upon by us, by the government, with all the social partners before. Of course, in addition to these measures, we need to further improve and optimise infrastructure – another important point for the quality of an economic location – which we have today. I have already had discussions with the social partners on this subject in the previous year, even in the pre-election period, with the Ministers responsible, whom I have invited. There have been negotiations which have led us, as Minister Ditz has already said, to have and benefit from longer-term investment opportunities outside the budget for the expansion of infrastructure, for rail transport, where it is necessary, in the field of road transport, telecommunications, environmental construction. This leads to an optimization of the quality of the Austrian economic location for the future. All of this was done in the joint agreement of the government with the economic partners. We decided not only now, but already in the previous year, otherwise it would have been far too late. [[Congratulations with the SPÖ and representatives of the ÖVP.]] These measures will not only ensure the quality of the economic location of Austria for the future and thus for all sectors of the economy, they will also help to restore the high level of unemployment currently in the construction sector, which is partly structurally dependent but also seasonally due to the long winter. We already had around 20 000 unemployed in April, less than at the end of March. With these measures for the expansion of infrastructure, which are now only beginning to take hold – in reality, they will only be fully effective in the course of this year and in the coming year – we will also be able to get back to unemployment in the field of the construction industry. This is of course also important for all other areas, because these investments have an impact not only on the construction industry, on the construction sector, but also on many other areas. But the decisive thing is not just this short-term measure of job security, but the decisive thing is that we continue to improve infrastructurally and thus further improve the quality of the Austrian economic location. [[Congratulations with the SPÖ and representatives of the ÖVP.]] Another fundamental condition for the high quality of the Austrian economic location is that we have a very, very stable currency, also due to the good cooperation of government and social partners. Austria is one of the world's countries with the hardest currency, and this, on the other hand, also leads to a very low inflation rate. But if we want to benefit from this advantage of high monetary stability in the future, it is particularly necessary that we find the way to monetary union. In other words, consolidation measures in Europe leading to monetary union are not negative for full employment policy but positive. For a country like Austria with a hard currency policy, it is necessary to prevent other countries from having short-term competitive advantages by devalues and other measures in the monetary field. The aim is therefore to find, within the European Union, a monetary union with these consolidation measures, which is important for Austrian employment policy and is also seeking to achieve. Reaffirming what Minister Ditz has already explained: Despite these consolidation measures, we have put great emphasis on the fact that the corresponding resources will continue to be available for employment security. The tax policy has already been called economic policy by Minister Ditz, and I cannot add anything to it. One question is: In future, how will we also sell what we can produce more and more in a shorter period of time? Here, too, it is necessary – and the government has already begun to negotiate with the social partners – to provide export support, especially for small and medium-sized enterprises, together with the chambers, together with banks, because small and medium-sized enterprises are of course unable to establish a marketing system worldwide. This is to be supported by us, because the better export measures represent a significant part of the opportunities for future employment policy. On this occasion, by the way, I would like to point out that the Economic Research Institute has determined that the opening of borders did not, as many believe, lead to job losses as a whole, but rather to the creation of more jobs by means of new export opportunities, much more so than, unfortunately, there has been a loss of migration. At the same time, I can see on this occasion that, because of the quality of Austria's economic location in recent years, very prominent companies which are examining their location very closely in international corporations have opted for Austria: BMW, Opel, Siemens, Hoffmann-La Roche, and, and, and. This list could continue for a long time. All these companies have come to Austria due to the good economic location policy. One thing is important: We also need to strengthen our support for small and medium-sized enterprises, and we need to support new business start-ups – this is also happening – but it is crucial that we keep industry in Austria, because only through this cooperation of industry with small and medium-sized enterprises, through a good and healthy mix between industry and small and medium-sized enterprises in the field of services and tourism, is it possible that we have implemented and will continue to maintain this high level of employment in Austria so far. [[Congratulations with SPÖ and ÖVP.]] It is also necessary that research is increasingly available to small and medium-sized enterprises with their results, that closer links be found between universities and the economy, not only in the field of research but also in the field of training. It is important in all areas that this economic orientation takes place even more. Despite budget consolidation, we did not cut the budget cut in the field of active labour market policy, but increased the funds. This year, around 1.5 billion Schilling will be more available for active labour market policies. Last year it was about 5 billion, this year it will be more than 6.5 billion schilling. These 6.5 billion schillings for active labour market policies are used about 70 percent for qualification measures, further training measures, re-training measures. And especially in the year of life-long learning, we must increasingly promote that people are even more willing than they are today to continue training in their professional field. [[Congratulations with the SPÖ and representatives of the ÖVP.]] The subject of reduction of working time is one in the long term. The subject of working time flexibility is a subject which is currently under discussion and which is also to be addressed. And there are very good discussions in this respect, which are being held by my place of business with the social partners. Only: Better design opportunities in the field of working time must benefit both businesses and workers. It is certainly not possible – and this is only sought by some, not by the majority of entrepreneurs, that I would like to say here – to see flexibility of working time only in such a way as to reduce overtime costs. It can't be. But more flexible working time models are very likely to be negotiated and will also come to a conclusion. Minister Ditz referred to the negotiations in the field of construction. These negotiations between the social partners are largely complete, and I expect them to be completed in the next 14 days, but in such a way that both parts, entrepreneurs and employees, find a reasonable system, a model which is beneficial to both sides. [[Congratulations with the SPÖ and representatives of the ÖVP.]] With regard to the flexibility of working time, it should be noted that in Austria we already have great opportunities for flexible design of working time, opportunities which are largely not yet exploited. We already have industry-oriented possibilities today, we also have the possibility – like any industrial state – of shifting, and we can say that in the field of shifting, we are currently discussing whether women can and can do night work in general. This problem is to be solved, even in the interests of women, and it is expected that by the summer a solution will be found which will also allow women access to night work. I deliberately say 'allowed' because many women are in difficulty because only men can be used in the shift, and especially in the last few weeks and months of the more often women have lost their jobs. That is why there will be a new regulation here, whereby women can do night work in many areas today, especially in the most difficult – I am thinking only of the hospitals, the transport sector and so on. Just one thing: Night work can never be equated with work on the day. Night work is more stressful, more stressful for mental reasons, for physical reasons, more stressful for health, more stressful for the family. This is why there must also be a balance, which goes beyond today's financial compensation arrangements. That is why I am also in favour of the fact that there is a time limit for night work in certain hours, since, of course, we have to provide for a proper transitional period, which can only be effective in a few years so that companies can continue their wage policy and so on. [[Congratulations with the SPÖ.]] I am therefore convinced that we will solve this problem of more flexible working hours in the interests of both parts and taking into account the interests of both parts, workers and employers, until the summer of this year. This is a step that we cannot expect to create a lot of jobs, but a step that is necessary to offer better conditions of competition in individual areas, but also to give workers more opportunities. The longer-term trend from my point of view can only be that: We must take into account that we can produce more and more in a shorter and shorter period of time. However, we must also take into account that basic training will be less and less long enough to require more and more further training, in all sectors, so that more of our lifetime needs to be made available for further training. Therefore, after the current discussion on more flexible design possibilities of working time must be the next discussion, which will certainly not be to be held from today to tomorrow, which will take some years: How do we find completely new ways in the more flexible design of the entire lifetime? We are happy to live longer, we can create what we need and sell in an increasingly shorter period of time, but we need more time for education. This flexible working time is therefore intended to ensure that more blocks are available for further training throughout the life, but more larger blocks are also available for leisure. For example, someone should be able to say at 35 years of age that he wants to be half a year for further education or he wants to have a few months of free time now. These systems must be developed. [[Congratulations with the SPÖ and representatives of the ÖVP.]] This is not going from today to tomorrow, because as simple as in the public service, where there is a continuous entrepreneur, these systems are not to be implemented – but they can be implemented. If you can arrange over-company holidays – for example, I refer to the building workers' holiday funds – it must certainly be possible in the future to take care of them in over-company leisure, over-time, holiday and treatment funds, which should then be able to pay more cheaply for further training from this account and to be charged for leisure time normally. These are the objectives of a real quality of life-based working time policy for the future. [[Congratulations with the SPÖ and representatives of the ÖVP.]] One issue that is currently being very discussed is the additional wage costs on which I would also like to comment. We have a good, socially balanced situation in Austria. The negotiations on wages have been conducted in a social partnership and have never resulted in any pressure. I therefore assume that they are also in line with the situation. In addition to the cost of social security, health care, health care, pension provision, the provision of pensions cannot therefore be abandoned, because at the same time the quality of our social security would be destroyed immoderately. Therefore, there can only be a change here if alternative means of financing are created at the same time. This is inseparable from one another, with many other areas outside the social system, of course: Is it useful – where it goes, should it be avoided – to calculate taxes from the wages? This is to be said about the additional wage costs from my point of view. That is to say, they cannot be deleted without further action, unless other measures are taken to ensure social security. [[Congratulations with the SPÖ and representatives of the ÖVP.]] In order to re-examine active labour market policies. Of course, we need to focus our entire policy – financial policy, economic policy and everything – on having enough employment. Of active labour market policies, however, over the next few months and years, there will be even more emphasis on economic-oriented education and training, with particular emphasis on the promotion of women so that they can find equal opportunities on the labour market. Special measures – of course, as before – must be taken for the disabled. In addition, we have our own concept of how we help long-term unemployed to return to employment. The main problem, however, is that the main focus for this year is for me the employment of young people. We have the lowest youth unemployment in all of Europe in Austria – and that must remain the case! [[Congratulations with SPÖ and ÖVP.]] We will therefore, together with the relevant ministers in the government and with the social partners, seek to further improve training. We will provide more information to young people about the professional information centres, and at the same time – this is new; there are also discussions with the Federal Minister Gehrer, who has also been working for her field of ministry in the EU – in the EU, we will seek to increase professional field research so that we can tell young people safely or at least more securely what occupations, which trainings have the future. The trend will be that basic training will be restructured in favour of good continuing training, showing young people from the very beginning the possibilities of continuing training. In addition, we will be increasing the resources for youth employment this year in the field of active labour market policy. We spent 200 million schillings in this area last year. We will spend around 600 million schillings this year, and we will work together with the social partners to ensure that every young person seeking training finds training this year too. We will try to ensure that Austria remains the country that has the lowest youth unemployment, because youth unemployment is not only for human, but also for social reasons the problem that we must avoid most. [[Congratulations with SPÖ and ÖVP.]] We – the Federal Government, the Länder and the Communities – have, together with the social partners, managed Europe's employment policy better than other countries. We will continue to do so in the future with optimism and with the new regulations we are making. These new regulations range from infrastructure development, training, further training to the reduction of unnecessary red tape. All of this will help to ensure that we do not, in the future, seek to achieve the goal of full employment, not with wage dumping, but with social dumping, with the quality of work, with the quality of export and with the quality of support from the Government and Parliament. Thank you.

Mr Haider has aged over the fact that people who spend overtime are treated so badly, and he thought that willing people would be harmed in such a way. – I say to you: What we're doing now is a negative steering effect. We make overtime so cheap that they actually push out other work. These overtimes no longer carry social burdens; they are much cheaper than limit hours. The path must be more reversed. You have to think seriously: On the one hand, the overtime supplements may not be too low, because they are easy to arrange overtime, and if you can find them – if you can get up the overtime supplements – you can not tax them fully and quite normally, because that would be fairer? [[Congratulations at the Liberal Forum.]] Then there would not be more hours left, but there would really be more where it is useful and correct for certain reasons. That all of this does not go without socially flanking measures, that it is not simply going to buy that, although people with little employment gain income, they cannot live on it – the United States is showing us this in one segment; in another segment, they are very successful, even in high-quality jobs – be only the completeness mentioned half of my time. For this reason too, we have considered. We have to redefine unemployment. That would be the real task that the Federal Government would now have to do. However, as long as one is always moving in the circle of those who have already worked, and all those who have a training, but may never really be able to enter the labour market or slow down or only very difficult, as long as one leaves it outside, of course the statistical figures are also automatically beautiful, because then all those who never had a job before are not counted. Then 4 per cent of unemployment rates are so low in us and 11 per cent higher in other countries because there may be a higher fluctuation there, so that just more people are caught in these statistics and unemployment is more visible there than we are; with us it is hidden. I think it is a serious mistake to beautify the unemployment rate in statistics in such a situation. Those who are unemployed know that they have no work and cannot be comforted at all by the fact that they are only a part of 4 percent published. There, someone who finds a job that is not enough in the sense of what I have done before receives payment. It will be discussed whether the Wiener Kabelgesellschaft should offer services via its cable networks. But what is not discussed – and that would be a worthwhile task for the Minister of Economic Affairs! – is what universal service we offer in Austria could develop what services it should integrate, what should be included in information, and whether this would be an intelligent, meaningful product for the future. We would have opportunities to develop something in a market, which could be exemplary not only for Austria but for Europe as a whole, an intelligent product that can be easily used by every Austrian. These would be the requirements for such a universal service in the field of information services as a whole. That would be possible and imaginable. This does not require the billions who spend large companies in the field of information technologies. One would come out with relatively modest means and would have an exportable product, a product that makes sense to people, because it would not only enable the previous users of information technology to use it, but because it would be a useful and useful technology for everyone to provide access to information that is not otherwise available. That would be useful and possible. But the situation in the media and information market is another – I do not happen to wear this T-shirt. [[Abg. The green wears T-shirts with the label "S.O.S. ==References==]] We are faced with a huge concentration in the media sector, an enormous poverty of ideas and a frightening degree of decreasing freedom of expression. We – and this is really significant for Austria – have a media concentration in the printing media in Austria, as it is hardly comparable to another European country. Switzerland is currently alone in the printing media sector 120, I think – I don't have the exact number – Newspapers. It is not the best European country in this respect, but the Scandinavian countries are even better: You have more newspapers. Switzerland is the third best European country in terms of the number of daily newspapers in relation to the population. Austria is at the level of a developing country, with regard to the number of daily newspapers, but not with regard to the number of readers. Ladies and gentlemen of the government parties! This is the result of your policy, which in recent years and decades has completely failed, which has subjected itself to a media cartel and which has in no way been willing to take any step against this media concentration, however necessary it would have been. So there are many new possibilities from the technical point of view. I very much believe that ministries are called upon to take initial steps in this regard, because it is not always possible to call for initiatives by private entrepreneurs. There are certainly also possibilities to extract and force telework in these areas. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP.]] We from ÖVP do not want wage dumping, but fair income for the self-employed! There are opportunities in many areas, and we are all invited to become active. In any case, I am not aware of the fact that only one job would have been created through debt allocations.

ParlaMint-AT_1996-05-07-020-XX-NRSITZ-00020_d7e9964	Mr President. Federal Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! ==References== In May of this year, the "Kammernachrichten Oberösterreich" read the following: To create work by reducing labour costs. – This article refers to a study by Professor Schneider of the University of Linz, in which it would be said that the cost of wages would be reduced by about 48 500 jobs per year in Austria until 2006. I believe that the strategy for reducing labour costs from an operational point of view may be all but negative, because the benefits exist only for so long until the competitors go after it. However, this short-term advantage is bought by the fact that working conditions and the standard of living of people deteriorate, the purchasing power declines, and sales opportunities decline. We cannot compete with the low-cost countries, which sometimes pay a tenth of our wages, about labour costs, otherwise we also have their standard of living. Instead of the defensive concept, the labour cost race, ladies and gentlemen, we need an offensive competition of know-how, quality and innovation. According to the credit protection association – which is also known – 86 percent of the insolvencys are homemade, i.e. indebted to the affected entrepreneur himself. Only about 10 percent of the loss sources are in the non-commercial sector. In an analysis of the electrical and electronic industries, company consulting firm Mc Kinsey showed that the key competitive weaknesses of European industry companies are not customer orientation, cumbersome processes, departmental segoism and employee management errors. Six of the currently leading business consultancy companies are facing major management deficits. Mc Kinsey sees the savings in material, energy, transport costs and efficient work organisation as much higher than labour costs. Similar calculations are also made from the German electronics industry. The key strategies for successful companies are therefore staff qualifications, much more attention being paid to marketing, innovation, production time and Committee reduction, and reduced and optimised product development by involving all stakeholders – from customer to supplier and all affected employees. Successful companies are consciously targeting their personnel policies to promote the motivation and skills of employees. I refer to an article in the "Economiesblatt" of 27. January of this year, where Werner Vontobel writes: "Streicheln rather than Streicheln". – In this article, he refers to a study result: The highest profits are achieved by companies that guarantee safe jobs and wages. The true recipe for success means job security, high wages and cooperation instead of confrontation. Vontobel also cites here a book by Professor Pfeffer of the Stanford Graduate School of Business, who took a close look at five US companies in a study. Professor Pfeffer also writes: "The five companies have neither a dominant competitive position nor a unique technology, nor could they be described as mass producers. Rather, it turns out that the decisive success factor was apparently in dealing with staff in a solemn manner. For example, the workforce of all five successful companies were trade unionally organized, all involved in the profits of the companies or the productivity progress of their departments." – Ladies and gentlemen! So there are also strategies that would be implemented in the future. A long-term study of German companies at the University of Hanover shows: Successful companies pay their employees on average higher wages than other companies in the comparison group. Now some rates on the additional wage costs, which have already been very strained today. I do not want to discuss the height anymore; that is known. The service providers have to pay social taxes and taxes of the wage sum of about 32 percent; if the holidays, sicknesses, holidays are included, you reach 66 percent. Only if holiday and Christmas money were counted as additional wage costs – which is wrong – would this figure increase to 96 percent. However, ladies and gentlemen – and this should be said again here – are not the additional labour costs, but the total labour costs related to productivity, namely unit labour costs, and in this respect Austria has very good values. Between 1980 and 1990, Austria's position on unit labour costs improved by 0.7 percent each year vis-à-vis its trading partners. Only in recent years has it increased slightly more than in comparable countries. In the last 15 years, productivity in Austria has increased by far more than our trading partners. The "Export World Champion" Germany also has 25 percent higher labour costs than Austria and has already fallen behind Austria in terms of productivity. That is, Germany has higher unit labour costs than Austria. I cannot address everything in detail, but one thing I would like to stress in this connection: Staff costs represent an increasingly low share of total costs. Ladies and gentlemen! Since today there is so much talk about additional wage costs: Consider how high the total staff costs are: They are only more than 15 to 35 percent, including additional wage costs; the trend is falling. They are always talking about the cost of wages, and we all know that in industry - it may already be that in some areas of services the share of labour costs is higher - in large areas and also in the trade of the share of labour costs is falling to 15 to 35 percent. In the so export-oriented saw industry, it was only 12.9 percent, in the non-ferrous metal industry, in other words in the multi-metallic areas and thus only more than 14 percent. Some colleagues and I were at an operating visit to BMW in Steyr yesterday. Do you know how high the wage cost is? – 9 percent! But here you are always talking about reducing the additional wage costs. – In reality, these are a very small part. That is why you should stick to the truth! Ladies and gentlemen! Being competitive – this is ultimately the result of a large number of complex factors, and these are not equivalent to labour costs. In a joint work, six of the currently leading business consultants have assessed the most important 16 location factors for them. The key factors include: skilled workers, high education, technical knowledge, high productivity, efficient management and a well-developed transport and telecommunications infrastructure. Austria's strengths lie in the good qualifications and motivation of employees, in social peace and in high quality of life and security! Finally, let me quote the "Economic Sheet" in which Werner Vontobel writes: "The economy is not as it is: It's the way you think it is. If everyone is convinced that the exchange rates are rising, they will rise. If everyone believes that the economy is a jungle in which only the strong and the careless can survive, then the economy is a jungle." The jungle theorists are still the loudest. Let us make sure that it will be different!

ParlaMint-AT_1996-05-07-020-XX-NRSITZ-00020_d7e10809	Mr President. Minister! Austria had a seasonally adjusted unemployment rate of 3.9% at the end of April 1996. The European Union had almost three times as high, 11 percent. This is undoubtedly a great success, a great success, although the increase in unemployment compared with the previous year should not leave us cold. I would like to speak in my speech, after today's very, very much talking about economy, businesses, economic power, dealing with people, namely, people who are free from work, free in a double sense: the unemployed. I believe that such a discussion is not suitable for a speculative discussion that the importance of employment can best be seen at its opposite, namely that of what is unemployment, namely simply distress, mental and physical distress, depression, social isolation, self-destructive guilt, most of which go hand in hand with it. That is, employment is a central life experience, is not only income security, is not only satisfying consumer needs, but jobs and jobs are important for life satisfaction, for confirmation as a source of social recognition and as a basis for social contacts. The social costs of unemployment are therefore always too high. You're always too high. They're called suicide, they're called alcohol and drug abuse, they're called crime and violence. This is clear from studies that the increase in unemployment is also accompanied by the rise in the rate of crime, particularly affected women, older workers, young people and ethnic minorities. If 35 million people are unemployed in the industrialized countries, equivalent to 8.5 per cent, and in some areas of Europe youth unemployment is 50 per cent and more, 50 per cent are too many. Unemployment is a socially undesirable phenomenon and must therefore be fought decisively as such. The transfer of capital and production without frontiers has brought people – regardless of their social level of development and culture – to compete with those who offer a much more favourable cost structure: with the countries of South-East Asia, for example. An Austrian with a 100 square meter apartment, with two studying children, with cars and everything that belongs to the Western standard of living, must compete with a Korean who lives on 30 square meters, rides the wheel to work and whose children themselves have to finance their studies. That means: The strengthening of research and also the concentration on services will not cover the progressive loss of jobs and the demigration of production to these emerging economies, and I would add that the exclusive beneficiaries of this situation are the owners of capital. [[Congratulations with the SPÖ.]] What I would argue for, because many of these problems are structural problems and today's workers are no longer comparable to previous skilled workers, and work is no longer the main productive force when companies replace human work with computer-integrated manufacturing systems, which produce better and more flexible, and thus learnt workers and auxiliary workers are driven out of the process: If only a few industrial workers can keep pace with the new working conditions to work in independent teamwork, to bear responsibility for a complex system of machines and robots and the other marginal workers, become temporary workers, at any time announceable, according to the needs of the enterprises, so if work results in some work and others no longer have work, then the work that leads to the elimination of work. That is, the central point, the central conflict, in my view, is that there must be a new catalogue of freedoms and rights: the right to learn, study at every age and not merely to reproduce its labour force, to recycle, to develop the right to public and political activities, the right to remain in sick relatives, the right to self-employment, the right to sovereignty of time. [[Congratulation with the SPÖ]] A reduction in working time is therefore essential, and that is what I mean not only in terms of daily or weekly working hours, but above all in terms of annual working hours, in terms of working time. If the economy offers a large part of the working population only more interrupted, temporary, half-day or precarious employment if precarious workers are paid only more for the work done, but not for time-disposability, and if between 70 and 90 percent of the small employees are women, then this is a cryous injustice. It is an injustice that a part of the population is working all day, while an ever larger part of the shorter time is working with lower wages and a part of it remains unemployed at all. In my view, therefore, employment must be reduced for all, because the individual has an untouchable right not only to income but also to work, a right to his own development. Unemployment can be understood as a temporary interruption of participation in economic activity, but not as an option not to be involved in the labour process at all. If a certain amount of work is necessary for the existence of society and its individuals – and this is given by the nature of things – then no one should bear the burden of necessity for others, no one should be exempted from it. I am therefore also skeptical of all the demands made by the Liberal Forum on Unemployed Basic Income, because it guarantees a right to income, but not the right to work, and the right to work is inseparably linked to the right to income. [[Congratulations with the SPÖ.]] In my view, the feeling of social belonging belongs not only to the right to income, but above all to the feeling that others need me to be able to do what corresponds to social goals. But it is tragic and sad that man is able to shoot himself at the moon, produce excellent technologies, but not to achieve an intelligent work organisation or a work distribution. If the volume of work required by society decreases and if unemployment increases, then the amount of work required by society must be redistributed. If the regular working time is falling and the sovereignty of time is increasing – and this in the case of increasing productivity – working time has to be redistributed. If one knows that around 1900, 3,000 hours of work were needed to produce one fifth of what is produced today in 1,600 hours of annual work, then one recognizes the need for socially compatible redistribution of time and work. In my view, this is the priority employment policy objective that must now be achieved in the sense of justice.

ParlaMint-AT_1996-05-07-020-XX-NRSITZ-00020_d7e11874	I would like to thank the rapporteur for his statement. The speaking time agreement has already been discussed. Mrs Fekter spoke. Please, ladies and gentlemen.
ParlaMint-AT_1996-05-07-020-XX-NRSITZ-00020_d7e11902	Mr President. Minister! My venerations Ladies and gentlemen! The now debated broker law codifies for the first time a large number of important economic activities in the field of mediation. We have standardised by this law a uniform clear regulation for all brokers and special rules for real estate agents, brokers, insurance brokers and personal credit intermediaries. The law lays down rights and obligations and brings more transparency and better consumer protection. A key concern of the broker is the acquisition of the commission in the event of successful mediation. The private legal system of a legal relationship must therefore ensure that compliance with performance and consideration is ensured. Therefore, consideration should be given to both the interests of the clients – which is mostly the consumer – and to the interests of the brokers in this central point. In this field of tension is the whole law to be seen. In order to safeguard these different interests, § 3 requires special reporting obligations, which are standardised in terms of mutual due diligence in contract design. It is not implemented in detail in the law, which is to be understood by it, and I would therefore like to quote some cases here as an example. In particular, the contracting parties will have to notify themselves if there is a second contract involving another broker in the case of a multiple mandate, or if the sale or rental of the mediation object has taken place in the meantime, or if the conditions change, such as price, dates or state of the contract, or if necessary documents for the legal transaction, such as opinions, plans, contracts, administrative orders, court judgments or income calculations are to be delivered. The provision of other information, such as on previous types of use, for example due to any contamination or excessive load in the near environment, may also be part of the message obligations. It is of course also necessary to inform about future, already foreseeable taxes, such as taxes and fees. From all these provisions you can see that we want to create more transparency with the broker's law and that we want to reduce the existing distrust on the market and help build a functioning brokering, spoken mediation market. The accession of Austria to the EU has led to a changed situation in the field of insurance mediation, but this is not taken into account in this draft. The EU's consumer protection policy towards the insurance industry has taken the way to reduce State supervision in favour of insurance intermediaries. The general terms and conditions governing the conciliation agreement are to be defined separately in a separate directive. I very much regret that in Austria we do not have a satisfactory market with sufficient competition, especially in the insurance sector. It will therefore also be necessary in the near future to implement the EU’s Insurance Conciliation Directive, based on the general provisions of the Agents’ Act, which we will adopt today, into a separate insurance intermediary law. However, in the case of all regulatory densities, it must not be ignored that a law merely lays down the framework, but cannot replace contracts between the partners. That is why I am in detail against an obligation to contravene and also against commissions; this is only to the follow-up commission that has already been addressed today. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP.]] For the sale of real estate, the new real estate agent law brings improved consumer protection: Retirement within a week, special information obligations and for certain contractual agreements compulsorily enforce the form of writing, for example for commission payments in the absence of conciliation. However, these rules are not found in the Realtor's Act, but because of their importance to consumers in the Consumer Protection Act. The broker law required a relatively intense discussion at the preparatory stage, as it concerns sectors of the economy in which there is a huge field of tension between the clients, the intermediaries and consumers. I hope that this codification will bring us closer to a functioning conciliation market. Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_1996-05-07-020-XX-NRSITZ-00020_d7e12335	Mr President. Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen! The aim was to integrate the real estate right in the Austrian legal order only partially and very fragmentedly, clearly and clearly into a law. Even this legal systematic reason justifys this new law. However, the need for this legislative project was also reflected in the very special, ever-increasing importance of the independent broker in an ever-increasing free market. For the functioning of a market, its broad transparency is essential both on the supply side and on the demand side. The broker, who specializes in a particular sector of the economy, provides comprehensive knowledge of these market conditions to the supplier and the consumer/buyer, thus promoting the results of the game of forces on the market for the individual market participant as well as for the economy as a whole. In order to make it easier for the consumer, who cannot have sufficient knowledge of the often complex product designs and legal instruments, to use the services of a broker and eliminate potential business risks for him, the legal situation for the consumer is significantly improved by the present legislative project. I repeat, in part, what has already been said: increased written formal obligation, concrete information obligations in consumer business, but above all also the new right of withdrawal in real estate transactions, which is intended to protect consumers from unwarranted legal statements. If the broker loses essential obligations of interest, his client will in future also be entitled to moderation of the commission claim in addition to any claim for damages due. This broker law also regulates, in particular, the legal conditions of an increasingly important profession: the insurance broker. The regulation of their rights and obligations is a very important legal step. The desire of insurance customers for advice from an independent broker increases in view of the hardly visible variety of insurance products offered today. We are only at the beginning of the EU wide opening of insurance markets, which will lead to an even greater differentiation of the offer. The specific standardisation of the insurance broker's obligations of interest will help to meet the customer's right to expert advice and to provide the best possible insurance protection. The compulsory catalogue of legislation in the future has already been included in the case law and teaching. However, even before today's decision-making, the intention of the formal notice in the series of insurance brokers has greatly increased the awareness of their sensitivity to their potential liability resulting from these obligations and the cover to be provided. It also considers the implementation of an EU recommendation, which through a registration obligation wants to achieve a clear distinction between the dependent and independent insurance mediation – according to our division: the agents and brokers. In my opinion, the Agents' Act is a good proof that clear civil law standards can have a positive preventive effect even before their entry into force. Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_1996-05-07-020-XX-NRSITZ-00020_d7e13125	I would like to thank the rapporteur for her statement. There's a word message. Mrs Wurm has spoken. – Ladies and gentlemen, you have the floor. Maximum speaking time: 16 minutes.
ParlaMint-AT_1996-05-07-020-XX-NRSITZ-00020_d7e13150	Mr President. Federal Minister! Mrs. State Secretary! ==References== In Lugano, the Convention on the Jurisdiction and Enforcement of Judgments in Civil and Commercial Matters, in short Lugano Convention, was concluded in September 1988 between the EU Member States and the EFTA States. It is largely in line with the European Convention on Jurisdiction and Enforcement, which was concluded in 1968, in Brussels in short, and is therefore also referred to as the parallel Convention. The Lugano Convention is already in force between 14 European States. In the following, I give a rough overview of the basic rules of this Convention. The Lugano Convention will not only govern the recognition and enforcement of foreign decisions but also the international competence of the Contracting States. In this respect, there is a direct competence in contrast to the indirect competence common in previous bilateral enforcement agreements, which must only be considered as a mere rule of assessment at the stage of recognition and enforcement in the second State. The Lugano Convention must therefore already be applied in the recognition process and, within its scope, replaces national jurisdiction, i.e. the rule of law, as well as bilateral enforcement agreements, provided that they do not have a specific further scope. The objective scope of the LGF includes, in accordance with Article 1(1) thereof, the scope of the LGV. 1 without regard to the nature of jurisdiction – whether commercial or labour jurisdiction, disputed or out of dispute – all civil and commercial matters. However, it does not cover the situation of persons, the matrimonial property law, inheritance law, insolvency proceedings, social security and arbitration. It is also necessary for the application of this provision that the defendant party, including companies or legal persons, is resident or established in the territory of a Contracting State. If the defendant lives outside a Contracting State, the international jurisdiction of the respective Contracting State shall be determined by its own autonomous laws. Except for those cases, only the cases of exclusive competence under Article 16 of the basic Regulation. The special responsibilities of the second section justify electoral courts outside the State of residence. They regulate both international and local competence. It should be noted that the jurisdiction of the place of performance, the place of jurisdiction for maintenance, the court of jurisdiction for court proceedings, the jurisdiction of the Court of Adhesive Cases and the jurisdiction of the place of establishment. Article 6 of the Convention contains a catalogue of electoral courts, which allow for actions to be brought before a court on a case-by-case basis in order to avoid conflicting decisions. In this context, it should be noted that the jurisdiction of the disputed party, the jurisdiction of the guarantee and intervention action, the jurisdiction of the opposition and the jurisdiction of the real estate business. Special protection provisions are laid down in Article 7 and the following for policyholders, in Article 13 and the following for consumers and in Article 5(1). 1 second sentence and Article 17(1) 5 for workers. It should also be noted that there is a need for special needs for workers to come to their right to take initiatives at EU level beyond the current Convention, as well as for the European Trade Union Confederation to begin negotiations with EFTA States as soon as possible, with the aim of bringing the rules of competence of the Lugano Convention into line with the Entrepreneurship Directive in order to enable a posted worker to complain to the employer on equal terms both in the employment state and in his home country. [[Congratulations with the SPÖ.]] Article 17 gives the parties of which at least one is resident in a Contracting State the opportunity to conclude agreements on jurisdiction. However, jurisdiction agreements are ineffective if they violate the protection provisions of insurance and consumer matters – or if there is a compulsory jurisdiction. In the case of individual contracts of employment, a jurisdiction agreement may only be made after the dispute has arisen. Title III of the Convention finally regulates the recognition and enforcement of judicial decisions, Title IV, those of public documents and procedural comparisons. In order to ensure a uniform interpretation of the Treaty, the EFTA States, in Protocol No. 2 on the LGF, to respect the case law of the ECJ and the case law of the Contracting States of the ECHR. On the other hand, the Contracting States of the Brussels Convention must observe the jurisdiction of the EFTA High Courts. Ladies and gentlemen! As you can see from my remarks, this legal matter is very complicated and bulky and will provide a very inexhaustible source of scientific disputes and practical application in the implementation of more than a dozen States involved. Despite these expected initial difficulties, the priority must be to create a single legal area for jurisdiction and enforcement in civil and commercial matters in an already realized economic area in which the four fundamental freedoms apply. This area of law, which, in addition to the EU, includes the EFTA States and which also opens up the possibility of participating in the reform states, separates Austria from many bilateral agreements, which are also very complex and complex in both case law and application. Ladies and gentlemen! In confidence that, after ratification of this Convention, the implementation of legislation in Europe for consumers and economic operators will become easier and more efficient, the Socialist Group agrees with this Convention.

With Austria's accession to the European Union, part of Austria's security control tasks have been transferred to the Luxembourg EURATOM security control authority and will be transferred by it in accordance with Regulation No. 3227/76. ParlaMint-AT_1996-05-07-020-XX-NRSITZ-00020_d7e13371	Mr President. Mrs. State Secretary! High house! Of course, the Austrian People's Party will agree to a treaty on the non-proliferation of nuclear weapons in Europe and the world. It is the countries Belgium, Denmark, Germany, Ireland, Italy, Luxembourg, the Netherlands and Austria which conclude this agreement. But I am right to ask the question: Where are other countries of Europe in this regard? Where are France, Great Britain, Russia, where Ukraine? And outside Europe, the question is: Where are the People's Republic of China, Pakistan, Iran, India and so on? In my view, this treaty is under three premises, which show the whole problem of a treaty among nations. The first premise is: What was real once remains possible forever. – This sentence comes from the teacher of the philosophers, from Baruch Spinoza, and he has since the 16th century. Since the US's first nuclear bomb detonated on July 1, 1945, it has also been absolutely valid in this field. In the period between 1945 and 1982, more than 900 nuclear warheads were inflamed. The hope that people would forget the knowledge of the manufacture of nuclear weapons is an absolutely unfounded and a very deceptive one. The use of humans was also tested in 1945. Since these trials, the damocles have burdened the atomic threat over all humanity. The second premise is: Nuclear weapons are high-level weapons. Nuclear weapons mean world power – and that was fully aware of the inventors of the first nuclear bomb in the USA from the beginning. There is no world power without nuclear weapons today, if you look closely at it. There are a number of states that want to upgrade atomicly or are already developing their first nuclear weapons. A particularly bad state occurs when political or economic changes make a state system unstable and the care of these weapons is no longer guaranteed to the extent that corresponds to their danger. Third Premise: The so-called peaceful use of nuclear energy provides new raw material for nuclear weapons every day. The plutonium produced in nuclear reactors is capable of forming critical mass for nuclear weapons, and as long as nuclear reactors are in operation, the construction material for nuclear weapons does not go out. If an atomic milestone, as it were, makes itself independent and withdraws from the laws of control, as in the case of Chernobyl, then a nuclear power plant itself becomes an atomic weapon. In addition, the high risks come from harmful miles, from poorly maintained miles and from miles whose funding is in question from the outset. It is significant that, according to a survey presented today by our Environment Minister, apart from Maria Rauch-Kallat, 90 percent of the Austrians are afraid of the nuclear installations in our neighborhood, for example in Mochovce, in Bohunice, but also in Krško. We therefore call for nuclear weapons to be produced and passed on only under the strictest controls. We must also be able to monitor and track the flow of materials precisely in connection with nuclear energy, even if we are aware that complete control is not at all possible in this matter. There are countries which, although joining international agreements, do not adhere to them, and there are countries which do not, from the outset, require such agreements, because they are not prepared to comply with these provisions and contractual agreements. Therefore, there can only be one goal for us – admittedly a very high one –: It should be a self-restriction of mankind as a whole to abandon nuclear weapons at all and also to replace the peaceful use of nuclear energy with other, better forms of energy. [[Abg. Wabl: Bravo!]]
