There are some who practice this and believe that this is the cleanest and most decent solution, however, not to mention that one can quite press the Stech clock, then go into office and do his political business instead of doing his official business. It should be said quite openly and honestly once. I think Mr Cap is a bit more honest. He knows that a solution here is very, very difficult, but basically one can find solutions, but quite certain professions simply exclude each other. Mr Khol! Not only in the field of a clubman and a clubwoman, there is an incompatibility with another profession, but also in other areas. Ladies and gentlemen! This is the delicate point that has been discussed so often in recent times. Mr Amon and Mr Maitz will remember the hustle and bustle that suddenly broke out in the Styria in connection with the carnations. In 1986, when I made my first press conference in connection with my unemployed income as a teacher, I said I deserve very much as a politician. [[Intrusion of the Abg. Schwarzenberger.]] Mr Schwarzenberger, we can still talk about this. The Styrian Constitutional Service has now issued an opinion confirming my attitude, but it is now about something quite different. At the time, I asked the government parties to change that. 1986 already! That's nine years ago. Now I've done this again, and in the Styria it was fertile now. There have suddenly appeared from various directions by the Styria MPs who have said: I don't want to take the salary anymore, I want to have a job somewhere. There was an attempt to create jobs. Of course, in very prominent cases one has not known what to do. Examples: Flecker and Strenitzer. You had to open a desk somewhere, it wasn't that easy. First, one had to go somewhere else, so that these politicians could go back to their profession. What they're doing there, I don't want to look at that more closely now, but: There's nothing going to help a stech clock either. But the good thing was that suddenly three or four representatives of the ÖVP said they wanted to go back to work so that they could get their salary properly. – Of course, then, the Styrian state government finds a job again for these nice people from the ÖVP. That's not a problem. Then, too, there was a freeman, to the surprise of many. He said: I was a financier, I have a reference that I naturally donate – to keep it, he cannot afford, even in the FPÖ. But that's where they said: No, I can't. – It was clear to me again what new game began. Just under the wonderful title: There must be no professional ban! That, of course, all politicians must continue to exercise their occupations and continue to sit in their offices. And for those who happen to belong to the ÖVP or SPÖ who happen to belong to a government party, one will also find a place to open a desk. I guess that's gonna be possible. And then we'll give him another stech clock, just for the parties who're supposed to come and see that. He can then explain to them: I always get in there when I go in and go out, and only that way is reckoned. And if there is less going on in Parliament, then I can plug more and earn much more. Ladies and gentlemen! This is not correct, this is a lazy egg, which is obviously to be hatched here, but which is not acceptable. [[Congratulation with the greens.]] Mr Stadler! To your primary doctor: I think that if, in her profession in which she is active, the control is possible and works properly, which ensures that she is fully engaged in her profession, then she must take up another profession from me. [[Abg. May. Stadler: You can take poison on it! It's a private hospital!]] If she believes she can work 24 hours a day, she should do it. But where we control ourselves more or less where political influence is possible, it will be problematic. This is the problem, and we want to avoid it. Ladies and gentlemen! Mr Cap has said that party tax and club tax, that is a private matter, similar to Mr Peter. You only spoke of club tax, because the party tax has been abolished by you liberals. You are, I believe, the best-earning Members at all. In the Green Group, party tax is not a party tax, but there is a public initiative fund. In the SPÖ it is a party tax, since one has always opted for this resolute kind of party tax. I don't know that very well with the other parties. However, it should be the case with the EIA, because they always cry. There has been a wonderful story in a committee room in which Members have assembled. A new Member of the Greens has been sitting there with Members of other parties, with a few colleagues from here, and they have said: Look, dear students, I'm actually getting only 22,000 p.m. I have to pay party tax and financepocals, because I have to donate there, I have to donate club tax, I have to pay district taxes. I therefore remain only 22 000 p. The next one came and said: I've only got 27 000 S salary. The students were all completely confused. – And then a new Member of Parliament, the Gföhler, gets into his bag, takes out his wage bill and says: You stupid G’s, something must have gone wrong! However, I have 53 000 S. Something's wrong. This must not continue, Mr Peter. You can do what you want with your money, but what is now party tax is not voluntaryly paid, but it will be reduced by obligation. Transparency is the only opportunity in the political debate, transparency also in the party donations, whether they come from the MEPs or from companies and industrial enterprises. As always, our proposal is excellent, with a few marks. [[Hey, hey, hey, hey, hey, hey, hey, ……]] I wish you a nice evening. - Thank you.
